    • ProcedureWhat each check does and does not establish, and how to contribute.
Source
Neutral status. The abc conjecture is not proved here. The project neither assumes that inter-universal Teichmüller theory is correct nor that it is wrong; a countermodel to a simplified abstraction is not a counterexample to the papers.

Status

Progress is measured by statements and their statuses. Statement fidelity (is the statement extracted and stated in Lean?) and proof status (is it proved, and under which hypotheses?) are reported separately.

Headline figures

From data/dashboard.json, with the captions of docs/DASHBOARD.md. The statuses behind the counts are written by hand in data/curated/; scripts/dashboard.py checks them against fixed rules and against the Lean inventory, not against the papers.

369Statements in the mechanical index (numbered and lettered)
242Curated parts and proof steps
18Curated unnumbered items of a section
629Statement records extracted by reading (statement status other than NOT_EXTRACTED)
10Statement records formalized in full
3Statement records proved in full, unconditionally
0Statement records proved in full, conditionally (named hypotheses)
168Fragments checked in Lean, unconditionally
53Fragments checked in Lean, conditionally
43Parents with at least one checked fragment (not counted as formalized)
65Records (statements and fragments) whose declaration is a definition, structure or other non-theorem typed in Lean (never counted as proved)
951Obligation entries (items of the obligations lists, open claims and notes alike)
649Records with at least one obligation entry
347Statement records with an ambiguity (status INTERPRETATION_AMBIGUOUS or a nonempty ambiguities list)
26Countermodel catalog of data/countermodels.json: countermodels (of a simplified abstraction unless the entry says otherwise)
16Countermodel catalog of data/countermodels.json: positive results
1Countermodel catalog of data/countermodels.json: lemmas
0Unauthorized axioms
0Proof placeholders (sorryAx)
0Validation errors of the records

A fragment is a Lean lemma that checks one step or one arithmetic piece of a statement of the papers. It is counted on its own; its parent statement is not counted as formalized or proved through it. Formalized in full means that one Lean declaration states the entire content of the record; proved in full means that, in addition, the declaration is a theorem accepted with the allowed axioms. Unauthorized axioms and placeholders are counted over the 89 modules of the compiled inventory data/lean_declarations.json.

Fragments and their parents

A fragment is a Lean lemma, or a Lean definition, that checks or types one step or one arithmetic piece of a statement of the papers. It has its own record <parent>.frag.<name>. The parent keeps its own status: it is never counted as formalized or proved through its fragments. A fragment that is a definition has no proof status of its own.

IUT1.Def3_1 IUT I, Definition 3.1

The parent keeps its own status: statement extracted, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.frag.partialConditions: IUT.PaperI.PartialInitialThetaConditions (structure typed in Lean, no proof status)
    The conjunction of the Lean predicates for the clauses of conditions (a), (b), (c) that Mathlib can state, for a number field F, a Weierstrass curve W with W.IsElliptic, a natural number l, a number field k with F a k-algebra standing in for F_mod, and a set S of finite places of k standing in for V^bad_mod.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Clauses of (a), (b), (c), page 61 and 62

    Not covered.

    k is not required to be the field of moduli F_mod; conditions (d), (e), (f); the SL_2 image and q-parameter clauses of (c); C_F, F_sol, fundamental groups.

IUT1.Def3_1.a IUT1.Def3_1.a

The parent keeps its own status: statement extracted, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.a.frag.sqrtNegOne: IUT.PaperI.SqrtNegOneMem (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    SqrtNegOneMem F: there is x ∈ F with x^2 = −1.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause '√−1 ∈ F' of condition (a), page 61

    Not covered.

    That F is a number field (a typeclass assumption elsewhere), the choice of F̄ and G_F = Gal(F̄/F).

IUT1.Def3_1.b IUT1.Def3_1.b

The parent keeps its own status: statement extracted, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.badSetOddNonempty: IUT.PaperI.BadSetOddNonempty (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    BadSetOddNonempty S: S is nonempty and 2 ∉ w for every w ∈ S.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause 'V^bad_mod is a nonempty set of nonarchimedean valuations of odd residue characteristic' of condition (b), page 61, relative to an arbitrary k

    Not covered.

    That k is the field of moduli F_mod of X_F, and that X_F has bad (multiplicative) reduction at the places of F lying over S (the latter is the separate fragment IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.multiplicativeOverBadSet). Page 61 does not require S to be the full set of bad places: it notes that X_F may also have bad reduction at places over V^good_mod, so no equality with the set of bad places is asked for.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.galoisDegreePrimeTo: IUT.PaperI.GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo k F l: F/k is Galois and [F : k] is coprime to l.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause 'F/F_mod is Galois of degree prime to l' of condition (b), pages 61 and 62, relative to an arbitrary k

    Not covered.

    That k is F_mod.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.multiplicativeOverBadSet: IUT.PaperI.MultiplicativeOverBadSet (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    MultiplicativeOverBadSet W S: at every height one prime v of the ring of integers of F lying over some w ∈ S, the minimal model of W has multiplicative reduction.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause that X_F has bad (multiplicative) reduction at the places over V^bad_mod, condition (b), page 61, relative to an arbitrary k

    Not covered.

    That k is F_mod; good reduction at places outside the preimage of S is not stated.

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.semistable: IUT.PaperI.SemistableAtFinitePlaces (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    SemistableAtFinitePlaces W: at every height one prime v of the ring of integers of F, the minimal model of W over the valuation ring of v has good or multiplicative reduction.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause 'X_F admits stable reduction over all v ∈ V(F)^non' of condition (b), page 61, read as semistable reduction of E_F

    Not covered.

    That the reading of stable reduction of X_F as semistable reduction of E_F is equivalent is not proved; all other clauses of (b).

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.torsionSix: IUT.PaperI.TorsionRational (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    TorsionRational W n: every point P of W over AlgebraicClosure F with n • P = 0 lies in the image of the base change map from the F-points. With n = 6 this is the clause on 2·3-torsion.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The clause 'the 2·3-torsion points of E_F are defined over F' of condition (b), page 62, with n = 6

    Not covered.

    All other clauses of (b).

IUT1.Ex3_2 IUT I, Example 3.2

The parent keeps its own status: statement blocked by mathlib, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Ex3_2.frag.frd_morphism_types: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.IsFrobeniusTrivialObj (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    For a functor P : C -> F_Φ: the pre-Frobenioid hypotheses (Φ a divisorial monoid on D; C, D connected and totally epimorphic); linear, isometric, metrically equivalent, base-isomorphism, base-FSM-morphism, base-isomorphic objects, pull-back morphism, base-equivalent, Div-equivalent, base-identity and Div-identity endomorphisms, O^▷(X) and O^×(X), pre-step, step, primary pre-step, co-angular, LB-invertible, morphism of Frobenius type, prime-Frobenius morphism; Frobenius-ample, Frobenius-trivial and isotropic objects and isotropic hulls. Linear morphisms and pre-steps are closed under composition; identities are isometric pre-steps; base-identity implies Div-identity (Remark 1.2.1). The identity functor of F_Φ as a functor to F_Φ, with Base, Div, deg_Fr given by the components of the triple.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Definition 1.1 (iv) (page 20), Definition 1.2 (i) to (iii) (pages 21 and 22), from Definition 1.2 (iv) the Frobenius-ample, Frobenius-trivial and isotropic objects and isotropic hulls (pages 22 and 23), Remark 1.2.1 third implication (page 24). The notion 'Frobenius-trivial object [FrdI] Definition 1.2' listed by the parent.

    Not covered.

    The other object types of Definition 1.2 (iv) (Div-Frobenius-trivial, quasi-Frobenius-trivial, metrically trivial, base-trivial, Aut-ample, perfect, group-like, Frobenius-compact, Frobenius-normalized, unit-trivial, Frobenius-isotropic and others), Definition 1.2 (v), the subcategories C^istr, C^lin, C^bs-iso, C^pl-bk, the other implications of Remark 1.2.1; tempered Frobenioids, split Frobenioids and all IUT I data of Example 3.2.

IUT1.Ex3_3 IUT I, Example 3.3

The parent keeps its own status: statement blocked by mathlib, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Ex3_3.frag.frd_elementary_frobenioid: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.elemFrobCategory (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    The elementary Frobenioid F_Φ of a functor Φ : Dᵒᵖ ⥤ AddCommMonCat: objects of D; morphisms A -> B are triples (φ_D, Z_φ ∈ Φ(A), n_φ ∈ N>=1); composition (ψ_D ∘ φ_D, φ_D^*(Z_ψ) + n_ψ · Z_φ, n_ψ · n_φ); identity (id, 0, 1); left and right identity laws and associativity proved as theorems; the category structure as a plain definition (no instance registered); the projection functor to D; Base, Div and deg_Fr of composites in F_Φ; the monoid F_M on M × N>=1 with (a1, n1)(a2, n2) = (a1 + n1 a2, n1 n2) and the noncommutativity of the standard Frobenioid F_{Z>=0}.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Definition 1.1 (iii), page 20, including the composition display and the definition of F_M and F.

    Not covered.

    Functoriality of Φ ↦ F_Φ in Φ; the identification of F_M with the endomorphism monoid of the one-object category F_{Φ_M} is only described in a docstring, not proved; the p-adic Frobenioids of IUT I Example 3.3.

  • IUT1.Ex3_3.frag.frd_monoid_classes: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.IsDivisorialMonoid (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definitions of classes of commutative monoids and of monoids on a category: sharp, integral, saturated, torsion-free, perfect, the characteristic M/M^± as a quotient monoid, characteristic type, pre-divisorial (integral, saturated, of characteristic type), group-like and divisorial (each under the pre-divisorial hypothesis), the relations a <= b and a ≼ b, irreducible and primary elements, characteristically injective morphisms, non-dilating endomorphisms, fiberwise-surjective arrows, FSM-morphisms, totally epimorphic categories, monoids on a category (conditions (a), (b) of Definition 1.1 (ii)) and divisorial monoids on a category.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Section 0, monoid conditions of page 11, relations and elements of page 12, FSM-morphisms of page 14, totally epimorphic categories of page 15; Definition 1.1 (i) and (ii), page 19.

    Not covered.

    The perfection M^pf as a construction, primes Prime(M), bounded subsets and suprema, monoprime monoids, characteristic, groupification and perfection of a functor Φ; the monoids ord(O^▷L)^pf and ord(Z^▷{p_v}) of IUT I Example 3.3 and everything else of that example.

  • IUT1.Ex3_3.frag.frd_monoid_examples: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.isCharacteristicTypeMonoid_of_isIntegralMonoid (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    Integral monoids are of characteristic type (erratum 30), so pre-divisorial is equivalent to integral and saturated. The additive monoids N, Q>=0 and R>=0 are divisorial; Q>=0 and R>=0 are perfect and N is not; N is torsion-free and ZMod 2 is not; Z is group-like and not sharp; WithTop N is not integral; WithTop Z is not of characteristic type; the constant functor with value N on Discrete PUnit is a divisorial monoid on that category. General criteria: a cancellative monoid is integral; every element of M^gp is a difference of images; a commutative group is saturated; a canonically ordered monoid is sharp; a linearly and canonically ordered cancellative monoid is saturated and divisorial.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Comments on Frobenioids I, item 30 (Definition 1.1 (i), page 19); nonvacuity of the monoid classes of page 11 and Definition 1.1 (i), (ii).

    Not covered.

    No example of a non-saturated monoid and none of a pre-divisorial monoid that is neither group-like nor sharp is given. No statement of IUT I is addressed.

  • IUT1.Ex3_3.frag.frd_remark_1_1_1: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.frobDeg_compose (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a functor P : C -> F_Φ and composable f : X -> Y, g : Y -> Z of C: Base(g ∘ f) = Base(g) ∘ Base(f), Div(g ∘ f) = Base(f)^*(Div(g)) + deg_Fr(g) · Div(f), deg_Fr(g ∘ f) = deg_Fr(g) · deg_Fr(f); identities have Base the identity, Div zero and degree one; deg_Fr of the n-fold composite of an endomorphism is the n-th power.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Remark 1.1.1, page 21 (the three displays), stated for every functor to F_Φ, so in particular for pre-Frobenioids.

    Not covered.

    No statement of IUT I is addressed.

IUT1.Ex3_5 IUT I, Example 3.5

The parent keeps its own status: statement blocked by mathlib, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Ex3_5.frag.frd_partial_frobenioid: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.PartialFrobenioidConditions (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    One definition for each of the clauses (i) (a), (i) (b), (ii), (iii) (a), (iii) (b), (iii) (c), (iv) (a), (iv) (b), (v) (a), (v) (b), (v) (c), (vi), (vii) (a), (vii) (b) of Definition 1.3, for a functor P : C -> F_Φ, and their conjunction with the pre-Frobenioid hypotheses.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Definition 1.3, pages 24 and 25, all clauses except (i) (c) and (iii) (d).

    Not covered.

    Definition 1.3 (i) (c) (equivalence (C^pl-bk)A -> D{A_D}) and (iii) (d) (equivalences of the categories of co-angular pre-steps under and over A with Order(Φ(A)) and its opposite); hence not the definition of a Frobenioid. Realified Frobenioids, Prime(C) (Theorem 6.4) and all IUT I data of Example 3.5.

  • IUT1.Ex3_5.frag.frd_partial_frobenioid_example: IUT.Prerequisites.Frobenioid.natPoint_partialFrobenioidConditions (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    The elementary Frobenioid F_N over the one-object, one-morphism category Discrete PUnit, with the constant monoid N and the identity functor, satisfies the pre-Frobenioid hypotheses and every clause of Definition 1.3 that is stated (all except (i) (c) and (iii) (d)). In this example a morphism is a pair (Z, n) with Z in N and n in N>=1; the isomorphisms and the pull-back morphisms are exactly (0, 1), the pre-steps are the morphisms with n = 1, the Frobenius-type morphisms those with Z = 0, every morphism is a monomorphism, an epimorphism, co-angular and a base-isomorphism, and every object is isotropic and Frobenius-trivial. This is an instance of Proposition 1.5 (i), whose hypotheses (Φ pre-divisorial, D connected and totally epimorphic) it meets; Φ is sharp, so Φ^char = Φ and the natural functor F_Φ -> F_{Φ^char} is the identity.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Frobenioids I, Definition 1.3 (pages 24 and 25): a verified example of every stated clause, jointly; Proposition 1.5 (i) (page 27) for the one example D = Discrete PUnit, Φ = N, restricted to the stated clauses.

    Not covered.

    Clauses (i) (c) and (iii) (d) of Definition 1.3 for the example (not stated in Lean); Proposition 1.5 for general D and Φ, its types (Aut-ample, End-ample, and so on) and parts (ii), (iii).

IUT1.Prop2_1 IUT I, Proposition 2.1

The parent keeps its own status: statement blocked by mathlib, proof not extracted.

  • IUT1.Prop2_1.frag.anabelioid_morphism: IUT.Prerequisites.AnabelioidMorphism (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definitions: a morphism X to Y of connected anabelioids is an exact functor (Mathlib's ExactFunctor: preserving finite limits and finite colimits) from Y to X; it is an isomorphism when that functor is an equivalence; a basepoint is an exact functor to finite sets; the fundamental group of a basepoint is the automorphism group of its functor.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The Geometry of Anabelioids page 10, Definition 1.1.2 (i) and (ii).

    Not covered.

    The 2-categorical structure (Remark 1.1.2.1); recovery of G as Aut of a fundamental functor, uniqueness of fundamental functors up to isomorphism (page 9), independence of the fundamental group of the basepoint (Remark 1.1.2.2), Proposition 1.1.4.

  • IUT1.Prop2_1.frag.connected_anabelioid: IUT.Prerequisites.IsConnectedAnabelioid (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definition: a category is a connected anabelioid if it is equivalent to B(G) for some profinite group G (with fixed universe levels). Theorems: B(G) is a connected anabelioid; every Galois category in the sense of Mathlib is a connected anabelioid (via Mathlib's equivalence with finite continuous Aut F-sets of a fiber functor F). Definitions: a bundled connected anabelioid; the full subcategory of objects connected in the sense of Mathlib's Galois categories.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The Geometry of Anabelioids page 9: Definition 1.1.1 and one direction of Remark 1.1.1.1 (a Galois category is a connected anabelioid).

    Not covered.

    The converse of Remark 1.1.1.1 (a connected anabelioid is a Galois category; Mathlib has no transport of GaloisCategory along an equivalence); agreement of Mathlib's connected objects with the definition on Semi-graphs of Anabelioids page 7; anabelioids that are not connected; Example 1.1.3.

  • IUT1.Prop2_1.frag.finite_g_sets: IUT.Prerequisites.FiniteGSets (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definition: B(G), for a profinite group G, is Mathlib's category of finite sets with the discrete topology and continuous G-action. Theorem: B(G) is a Galois category in the sense of Mathlib (proof by Mathlib).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The Geometry of Anabelioids page 9, the definition of B(G) and the sentence calling it a Galois category.

    Not covered.

    That B(G) is a topos; the choice of a universe V and a V-small category of finite sets (replaced by Lean universes).

  • IUT1.Prop2_1.frag.semi_graph_of_anabelioids: IUT.Prerequisites.SemiGraphOfAnabelioids (structure typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definition: a semi-graph of anabelioids over a semi-graph assigns a connected anabelioid to each vertex and each edge and, to each branch b of an edge e abutting to a vertex v, a morphism of anabelioids from the anabelioid of e to that of v, that is, an exact functor from the anabelioid of v to that of e. Example: on any semi-graph, the constant semi-graph of anabelioids with every constituent B(G) and identity branch functors.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Semi-graphs of Anabelioids page 22, Definition 2.1 (a) to (c): the object G of the discussion preceding Proposition 2.1 of IUT I (page 44), without the PSC-type condition.

    Not covered.

  • IUT1.Prop2_2.frag.semi_graph: IUT.Prerequisites.SemiGraph (structure typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definition: a semi-graph is a type of vertices, a type of edges, a type of branches with a map to edges whose every fiber has exactly two elements, and a coincidence map sending each branch to the vertex it abuts to or to none. Further definitions: an edge abuts to a vertex; verticial cardinality; graphs; finite and countable semi-graphs; components; an edge joins two vertices; closed, open and isolated edges; coverticial edges; locally finite and untangled semi-graphs; the incidence graph and connectedness through it; joints. Lemmas: the branches of an edge form a finite type; verticial cardinality is at most two; in a graph every edge abuts to a vertex.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Not covered.

    The topological space of a semi-graph and the proof that connectedness of the incidence graph is its connectedness; trees; subjoints. Of Proposition 2.2: PSC-type, fundamental groups, decomposition subgroups, commensurable terminality.

  • IUT1.Prop2_2.frag.semi_graph_examples: IUT.Prerequisites.SemiGraph.exampleJoint_isJoint (theorem, proof not extracted)

    Definition: a morphism of semi-graphs consists of maps on vertices, edges and branches, a branch of e going to a branch of the image of e, injective on the branches of each edge, and compatible with the coincidence maps on branches that abut to a vertex; identity and composition; proper morphisms (preserving verticial cardinality), immersions and excisions (injective, respectively bijective, on branches abutting to each vertex), graph-coverings (proper excisions).     Definition: the sub-semi-graph of a semi-graph with given sets of vertices and edges, whose branches are those lying on the chosen edges and whose coincidence map keeps a vertex when it is chosen and gives none otherwise; its inclusion morphism; the maximal subgraph (all vertices, closed edges only).   • IUT1.Sec0.TemperedGroup.frag.tempered_group: IUT.PaperI.IsTemperedGroup (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definition: a group P with a topology is tempered if there exist a nonempty directed preorder I (in the universe of P), countable groups G i with the discrete topology, surjective homomorphisms G j -> G i for i <= j satisfying the identity and composition laws, and an isomorphism of topological groups from P onto the subgroup of compatible families of the product of the G i with the subspace topology of the product topology. Lemmas: invariance under isomorphism of topological groups; a countable discrete group is tempered; a group isomorphic to the limit of a surjective system of finite discrete groups is tempered; a tempered group is totally disconnected; the circle group is not tempered.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Not covered.

    B^temp(Pi), connected temperoids, the equivalence C = (C^0)^T, Galois-countability and the reconstruction of Pi and of pi_1(C), pi_1(C^0); that Z_p or any specific infinite profinite group is tempered.

  • IUT3.Prop3_9.i.frag.wpkt_haar_comparison: IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.wPkt_weight_eq_haar_iff (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a Q_p-algebra isomorphism G from a finite Q_p-algebra W onto prod_t of the summand packets, the dimension-normalized Haar log-volume of W on G^{-1}(prod_t X_t) is sum_t (1/dim W) L_t(X_t) with dim W = (sum_v e f)^|A|. The page's weight is w_t = (1/dim W) prod_alpha (ebar / e(t alpha)), ebar = sum e f / sum f; the weights coincide for all t (with A and V nonempty and positive degrees) iff e is constant, and then the two log-volumes agree on direct product regions; in general they differ by sum_t (w_t - 1/dim W) L_t(X_t); on regions p^{k_t} (R_E)^~_t the coefficients of -log p are sum_t w_t k_t dim_t and sum_t k_t dim_t / dim W, numerically 1/2 versus 1/3 for e = (1,2), f = (1,1), k = (1,0).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Comparison of the weights of page 94 with the dimension normalization of the whole packet (Packets.lean); both pass the tests of page 115.

    Not covered.

    The decomposition G (Proposition 3.1 (i)) is an input, not constructed. Not a statement of the papers. The comparison is made in the reading of IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.WeightedPacket, where the weights of page 94 multiply unnormalized summand log-volumes (see the ambiguities of IUT3.Prop3_9.i.frag.wpkt_logvol_def); the pages do not decide that reading.

  • IUT3.Rem3_9_5.i.frag.hull_deg_automorphisms: IUT.Countermodels.HullDegree.hullDeg_shear (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    Every continuous Q_p-linear automorphism h of V with measure(h O) = measure(O) preserves mu^log of every set. Multiplication by a unit g of V with |g_s| = 1 for all s and every permutation of the coordinates of K^I preserve both mu^log and mu^log of hulls. The shear (x, y) -> (x + y, y) of Q_p^2 maps O onto O and preserves mu^log of every set, but for U0 = Z_p x p^-1 Z_p: mu^log(U0) = mu^log(hull U0) = (log p)/2, while the image of U0 has mu^log = (log p)/2, hull p^-1 O and mu^log of the hull = log p.

  • IUT3.Rem3_9_5.i.frag.hull_deg_monotone: IUT.Countermodels.HullDegree.hullDeg_rigid_le_hull (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For every region U of V = prod_s L_s (discretely valued proper factors, Haar measures): mu^log(U) <= mu^log(pktHull U); equality holds iff U and pktHull U have the same product measure, and, for null-measurable U, iff pktHull U minus U is a null set. If U is contained in lambda O and every coordinate bound |lambda_s| is attained on U, then pktHull U = lambda O; in particular pktHull (lambda O) = lambda O. The monotonicity is also stated for finite extensions of Q_p with the spectral norm.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 127, Remark 3.9.5 (i): the hull contains the region; here the consequence for log-volumes and the exact equality case are checked.

    Not covered.

    Tensor packets, log-shells, the archimedean places, the averaged log-volume over places and labels of Corollary 3.12, and the regions that arise in its proof (unions of possible images under (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3)) are not modeled; the statement says nothing about them.

  • IUT3.Rem3_9_5.i.frag.hull_deg_union_principal: IUT.Countermodels.HullDegree.hullDeg_hull_union_principal (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For two sets lambda O, lambda' O: the hull of their union is m O with |m_s| = max(|lambda_s|, |lambda'_s|); their intersection is the box of the coordinatewise minima; measure of the union plus measure of the minimum box equals the sum of the two measures. For finite extensions of degrees d_s (spectral norm): mu^log(hull) = (sum_s d_s log max(|lambda_s|, |lambda'_s|)) / sum_s d_s. On Q_p^n: mu^log(union) = log(prod |lambda_s| + prod |lambda'_s| - prod min(|lambda_s|, |lambda'_s|)) / n, against mu^log(hull) = (sum_s log max) / n. On the sets lambda O, mu^log(hull) = mu^log, so equal mu^log gives equal mu^log of hulls.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 127, Remark 3.9.5 (i): the hull as the smallest lambda O, computed for a union of two such sets.

    Not covered.

    Tensor packets, log-shells, the archimedean places, the averaged log-volume over places and labels of Corollary 3.12, and the regions that arise in its proof (unions of possible images under (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3)) are not modeled; the statement says nothing about them.

IUT4.Cor2_2.i IUT4.Cor2_2.i

The parent keeps its own status: statement extracted, proof blocked by external theorem.

  • IUT4.Cor2_2.i.frag.frey_minimal_odd: IUT.FreyReduction.freyRedModel_isMinimal (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    Over the fraction field of any discrete valuation ring, an integral Weierstrass equation whose discriminant or c4 has valuation 1 is minimal (Mathlib IsMinimal), and two minimal equations related by a change of variables have discriminants of the same valuation. For coprime a + b = c and an odd prime p, the Frey model y^2 = x(x - a)(x + b), base changed from Z to Q_p, is integral over Z_p and minimal at p: if p does not divide abc its discriminant 16(abc)^2 is a unit, otherwise its c4 = 16(a^2 + ab + b^2) is a unit.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The pages read do not name a triple, the Frey curve, its reduction at odd primes, or the relation v(q) = -v(j); this is classical arithmetic (Silverman) checked for the rational points a/c of the lambda-line coming from coprime triples, through the Frey curve y^2 = x(x - a)(x + b), a quadratic twist of the Legendre curve of page 41. It checks the minimality at odd primes on which the q-parameter orders at the primes not dividing 2 (log q^{not 2}, Corollary 2.2 (i), page 41) rest.

    Not covered.

    Number fields other than Q; points of the lambda-line not coming from triples; the Legendre model at lambda itself; the prime 2; every assertion of Corollary 2.2 (i), in particular the equalities of bounded discrepancy classes. Nothing is asserted for the Legendre model over Q at the point of the triple: minimality, reduction type, discriminant and conductor change under the quadratic twist relating it to the Frey curve. Counted as a model test: the pages read do not mention a triple, the Frey curve or its reduction; this is classical arithmetic behind the invariants of Corollary 2.2, as the record says.

  • IUT4.Cor2_2.i.frag.frey_prime_two: IUT.FreyReduction.freyRedModel_two_not_isMinimal (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    At p = 2 the Frey model over Q_2 has neither good nor multiplicative reduction in Mathlib's sense (Delta and c4 are divisible by 16), and for the triple 3 + 16 = 19 the model y^2 = x(x - 3)(x + 16) is not minimal over Z_2: the change of variables with u = 2, r = t = 0, s = 1 gives the integral equation y^2 + xy = x^3 + 3x^2 - 3x.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The pages read do not name a triple, the Frey curve, its reduction at odd primes, or the relation v(q) = -v(j); this is classical arithmetic (Silverman) checked for the rational points a/c of the lambda-line coming from coprime triples, through the Frey curve y^2 = x(x - a)(x + b), a quadratic twist of the Legendre curve of page 41. It shows why the prime 2 is separated from the others, as log q^{not 2} is in Corollary 2.2 (i), page 41.

    Not covered.

  • IUT4.Cor2_2.i.frag.frey_reduction_type_odd: IUT.FreyReduction.freyRedModel_hasMultiplicativeReduction_iff (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For coprime a + b = c and an odd prime p, the Frey model over Q_p has multiplicative reduction at p (Mathlib HasMultiplicativeReduction over Z_p) exactly when p divides abc, good reduction (HasGoodReduction) exactly when p does not divide abc, and never additive reduction.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    The pages read do not name a triple, the Frey curve, its reduction at odd primes, or the relation v(q) = -v(j); this is classical arithmetic (Silverman) checked for the rational points a/c of the lambda-line coming from coprime triples, through the Frey curve y^2 = x(x - a)(x + b), a quadratic twist of the Legendre curve of page 41. It checks, for the Frey twist over Q (not for E_F over F), that the reduction at the odd primes is good or multiplicative. The paper obtains stable reduction of E_F at every nonarchimedean prime of F from Proposition 1.8 (v) (page 42), which this fragment does not address.

    Not covered.

  • IUT4.Cor2_2.i.frag.frey_val_disc_j_odd: IUT.FreyReduction.freyRedModel_val_j (theorem, proof kernel verified)

    The Frey curve y^2 = x(x - a)(x + b) as a WeierstrassCurve; Delta = 16 a^2 b^2 (a + b)^2 and c4 = 16 (a^2 + ab + b^2); over a field with 2 != 0 it is elliptic exactly when a, b, a + b are nonzero; its j-invariant is 2^8 (a^2 + ab + b^2)^3 / (a^2 b^2 (a + b)^2) = legendreJ (a/(a + b)) = legendreJ (-b/a); if u^2 = a with u != 0, the change of variables with unit u and r = s = t = 0 carries it to the Legendre curve at -b/a.

  • IUT4.Cor2_3.frag.frey_j_arithmetic: IUT.LegendreFrey.legendreJ_triple_prime_dvd_den_iff (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For coprime natural numbers a, b with N = a^2 + ab + b^2: N is odd and coprime to ab(a + b). For positive coprime a, b with a + b = c and j = legendreJ (a/c) in Q: j = 2^8 N^3 / (abc)^2; there is k dividing 2^8 with (abc)^2 = den(j) k and |num(j)| k = 2^8 N^3; den(j) divides (abc)^2 and (abc)^2 divides 2^8 den(j); N^3 divides |num(j)| and |num(j)| divides 2^8 N^3; an odd prime divides den(j) exactly when it divides abc. For the integral Frey model over Z, an odd prime divides Delta exactly when it divides ab(a + b), and then it does not divide c4.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    No page read names a triple or a Frey curve. For the specialization X = P^1, D = {0, 1, infinity} with the point a/c of a triple (module IUT.Applications.ABCSpecialization), this fragment checks the arithmetic of the j-invariant at that point and of the integral Frey model: coprimality, numerator and denominator up to powers of 2, and the odd primes dividing the discriminant.

    The pull-back of arithmetic divisors along a finite extension L/K of number fields: the additive map ADiv(K) -> ADiv(L) whose coefficient at a finite place w of L is e(w|v) times the coefficient at the place v of K below w, and whose coefficient at an archimedean place w of L is the local degree [L_w : K_v] (Mathlib's InfinitePlace.inertiaDeg, 1 or 2) times the coefficient at the place v of K below w.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 22, Definition 1.9 (i): the pull-back a|_K in ADiv_R(K) of a to a finite extension, 'defined in the evident fashion'.

    Not covered.

    This is a definition: the kernel checks only that it is well typed. The multipliers are not printed on page 22 nor on [GenEll] page 4; they are derived from the requirement that principal divisors pull back to principal divisors (checked by degPb_pullback_principal). The identification with the pull-back of arithmetic line bundles through ADiv/APrc = APic of [GenEll] page 4 is not formalized.

  • IUT4.Def1_9.frag.pullback_principal: IUT.PaperIV.DegreePullback.degPb_pullback_principal (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    The pull-back to L of the principal divisor of x in K^x is the principal divisor of the image of x in L^x: ord_w(x) = e(w|v) ord_v(x) (Mathlib's valuation_liesOver) and [K_v : R][L_w : K_v] = [L_w : R] with |x|_w = |x|_v.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 22, the pull-back 'defined in the evident fashion', checked against the principal divisors of [GenEll] page 4; this pins the multipliers of degPbPullback independently of the degree.

    Not covered.

    The pull-back of arithmetic line bundles of [GenEll] Definition 1.1 (ii), page 3. Counted as a model test: a check, made here, of the interpretation of the pull-back 'defined in the evident fashion'; the pages read print no statement on pull-backs of principal divisors.

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.bridge_ramification: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.padicBridge_uniformizer (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    The ring of integers R of L is local with maximal ideal {|x| < 1}, a discrete valuation ring; its maximal ideal lies over pZ_p and is the only prime over pZ_p. For such a prime P with e = P.ramificationIdx Z_p there is an irreducible w of R with P = (w), (p) = (w)^e as ideals, 0 < |w| < 1, |w|^e = 1/p, |x| = |w|^k (k an integer) for every nonzero x of L, and |x| <= |w| when |x| < 1. The image of the norm on L^x is {p^(k/e) : k integer}; |x|^e <= 1/p for |x| < 1, and e is the least natural number with this property.
    Covers and does not cover

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.ext_log_def: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.extLogOnePlus (definition typed in Lean, no proof status)
    Definitions in a normed field K: the logarithm series log(1 + x) = sum over n >= 1 of (-1)^(n+1) x^n / n (a tsum, a junk value where it does not converge), and the logarithm of units extLogUnit u = N^(-1) log(u^N) with N the least positive exponent such that |u^N - 1| < 1 (junk value 0 if none).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Not covered.

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.ext_log_hom: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.extLogOnePlus_add_add_mul (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a prime p and a complete normed field K that is a normed Q_p-algebra: the series converges for |x| < 1, equals NormedSpace.log (1 + x), and satisfies log(1 + (x + y + xy)) = log(1 + x) + log(1 + y) for |x|, |y| < 1; hence log((1 + x)^n - 1) = n log(1 + x). The logarithm of units satisfies extLogUnit u = N^(-1) log(u^N) for every N > 0 with |u^N - 1| < 1, extLogUnit(1 + x) = log(1 + x) for |x| < 1, extLogUnit(u^m) = m extLogUnit u and extLogUnit(uv) = extLogUnit u + extLogUnit v when u^N, v^N lie in the open disc around 1; in a locally compact K every u with |u| = 1 has such an N, and the least one is prime to p when |u| <= 1.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 11, proof of (i): the appeal to the well-known theory of the p-adic logarithm ([Kobl], p. 81), the homomorphism property and the extension to units, for a finite extension of Q_p in the normed-field model.

    Not covered.

    The inclusions themselves (fragment i_model); tensor products and (ii) to (iv).

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.ext_log_isometry: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.extLogOnePlus_norm_eq (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a prime p and a complete normed field K that is a normed Q_p-algebra: |log(1 + x)| = |x| when p |x|^(p-1) < 1, a condition equivalent to |x| < p^(-1/(p-1)); |log(1 + a) - log(1 + b)| = |a - b| on that disc, so x -> log(1 + x) is injective there; at the boundary p |x|^(p-1) = 1 the p-th term has norm |x|; for 0 <= r with p r^(p-1) < 1, x -> log(1 + x) maps the closed ball of radius r onto itself (surjectivity by the contraction mapping principle).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 11, proof of (i): the properties of the p-adic logarithm on the disc |x| < p^(-1/(p-1)) on which it is an isometric bijection (the role of the exponential in [Kobl], p. 81).

    Not covered.

    The exponential series itself; that the isometry fails at the boundary in a suitable ramified field (only the failure of the termwise estimate is shown); the inclusions of (i).

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.i_Qp: IUT.Prerequisites.PadicLog.padicLogUnit_range (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    Proposition 1.2 (i) for k_i = Q_p (e_i = 1, R_i = Z_p), with log on Z_p^x taken as (p - 1)^(-1) log(u^(p-1)): log(Z_p^x) is the closed ball of radius 1/p* in Q_p (p* = p for odd p, 4 for p = 2). Corollaries in the lemmas: for p != 2 it is the closed ball of radius 1/p, that is p Z_p (a = 1, b = -1, with equalities as the paper asserts for p > 2 and e_i <= p - 2); for p = 2, the closed ball of radius 1/4 is contained in it and it is contained in the closed unit ball, that is 4 Z_2 in log(Z_2^x) in Z_2 (a = 2, b = 0).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 10, display (i): p^(a_i) R_i in log_p(R_i^x) in p^(-b_i) R_i, with equalities for p > 2 and e_i <= p - 2, in the case k_i = Q_p (e_i = 1).

    Not covered.

    Ramified or nontrivial finite extensions k_i; assertions (ii) to (iv). For p = 2 the Lean result is sharper than the paper's inclusions (it proves log(Z_2^x) = 4 Z_2).

  • IUT4.Prop1_2.frag.i_model: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.extLogUnit_image_bounds (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a prime p, a complete, locally compact normed field K that is a normed Q_p-algebra, and a positive integer e such that |x|^e <= 1/p whenever |x| < 1: {z : |z| <= p^(-a)} is contained in extLogUnit({u : |u| = 1}), which is contained in {z : |z| <= p^b}, with a = Proposition1_2.a p e and b = Proposition1_2.b p e. The first inclusion holds for every e >= 1 without the norm condition. The same holds for every finite extension L of Q_p carrying a norm that extends the p-adic norm. In particular this holds for every field L with a finite-dimensional Q_p-algebra structure (the classes of the module IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Padic) equipped with Mathlib's spectral norm spectralNorm Q_p L.

  • IUT4.Prop1_3.frag.i_tame_equality_padic: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentExponent_eq_of_not_dvd_ramificationIdx_padic (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a finite extension L of Q_p and a prime P of its ring of integers over p with ramification index e: if p does not divide e, the exponent of P in the different (differentExponent Z_p R P) equals e - 1; equivalently differentOrder Z_p R P = (e - 1)/e. Proof: pR = P^e; the trace of R/P^n over F_p is n times the trace of R/P (additivity of the trace along 0 -> R/P -> R/P^(n+1) -> R/P^n -> 0); R/P is separable over the finite field F_p, so some trace is nonzero, and since p does not divide e some element of R has a unit trace in Z_p, which forbids P^e from dividing the different.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Pages 11 and 12, Proposition 1.3 (i): the equality d_i = d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i when k_i is tamely ramified over k_0, for k_0 = Q_p.

    Not covered.

    Proposition 1.3 (i) over a general base k_0 (needs the transitivity of the different over R_0 and ramification in towers); Proposition 1.3 (ii).

  • IUT4.Prop1_3.frag.i_wild_inequality_padic: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.ramificationIdx_le_differentExponent_of_dvd_padic (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    For a finite extension L of Q_p and a prime P of its ring of integers over p with ramification index e: if p divides e, then e <= differentExponent Z_p R P; equivalently 1 <= differentOrder Z_p R P, strictly above the bound (e - 1)/e. Proof: every trace from R to Z_p is divisible by p (it is e times a residue trace modulo p), so P^e divides the different.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Pages 11 and 12, Proposition 1.3 (i): the inequality is strict when k_i is wildly ramified over k_0 = Q_p (the paper states only the inequality and the tame equality).

    Not covered.

    Proposition 1.3 (i) over a general base k_0 (needs the transitivity of the different over R_0 and ramification in towers); Proposition 1.3 (ii).

  • IUT4.Prop1_3.frag.tower_ii_exponent_bound: IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.diffBound_prop1_3ii_of_classical (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    Proposition 1.3 (ii) for every finite L/K (no Galois or tameness hypothesis), through the classical upper bound for the different: the hypothesis v_P(D_{L/K}) <= e_{L/K} - 1 + e_L v_p(e_{L/K}) of towerProp1_3ii_of_exponent_bound is discharged by diffBound_exponent_le, giving d_L <= d_K + n + 1/e_K with n = multiplicity p [L : K].
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 12, Proposition 1.3 (ii), conclusion, for every finite L/K, through the classical upper bound for the different.

    Not covered.

    Setting: k_0 = K, k_i = L finite extensions of Q_p; R_0 = integralClosure Z_p K, R_i = integralClosure Z_p L, R_K -> R_L given by towerAlgebra; e_0 = ramificationIdx of P_0 over pZ_p, e_i = that of P, e_{i/0} = ramificationIdx of P over R_K (e_i = e_0 e_{i/0} by towerRamificationIdx_eq_mul); d = differentOrder (exponent of the different divided by the ramification index, ord(p) = 1); tame means p does not divide e_{i/0} (finite residue fields); n_i = multiplicity p [L : K]. The classical upper bound for the different is proved in IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.DifferentBound (IUT4.Prop1_3.frag.different_upper_bound); it is not a statement of the IUT papers and no page of a source for it was read. Counted as a model test so that its conclusion, checked as IUT4.Prop1_3.frag.ii_all_finite, is counted once; this record documents the route through the classical upper bound for the different, which is not printed on the pages read (page 12 proves (ii) through Kummer extensions).

    Laws of the normalized log-volume: mu^log(p^n . A) = mu^log(A) - n log p for n an integer, when A and Lambda have positive finite measure and d > 0; also mu^log(Lambda) = 0, mu^log(c A) = mu^log(A) + log|c|, translation invariance, monotonicity on sets of positive finite measure, independence of the Haar measure and of a continuous linear change of coordinates; the EReal form is monotone and equals the real form on sets of positive finite measure.

IUT4.ThmA IUT IV, Theorem A

The parent keeps its own status: statement extracted, proof blocked by external theorem.

  • IUT4.ThmA.frag.bounded_degree_implies_abc: IUT.VojtaNumberField.degreeAtMostOne_iff_ABC (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    The degree one piece VojtaLineMinusThreePointsDegreeAtMost 1 is equivalent to VojtaLineMinusThreePointsRational, hence to Challenge.ABC; so VojtaLineMinusThreePointsBoundedDegree, and VojtaLineMinusThreePointsDegreeAtMost d for every d >= 1, imply Challenge.ABC (for d = 0 the statement is vacuous). The transport for fields of degree 1 uses the height of a rational number in any number field (heightNF_algebraMap_rat), invariance of the log-conductor and of the discriminant under ring isomorphisms, and the computation of the three functions at rational points (heightNF_rat, logDiffNF_rat, logCondNF_rat).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 3, the sentence after Theorem A: 'the so-called Vojta Conjecture for hyperbolic curves, the ABC Conjecture, and the Szpiro Conjecture for elliptic curves all follow as special cases of Theorem A', for the ABC Conjecture through the bounded degree statement for P^1 minus {0, 1, infinity} at d = 1.

    Not covered.

    Theorem A itself and its proof; the converse (Challenge.ABC does not by this file imply the statement for d > 1); the general equivalence with Vojta's conjecture for hyperbolic curves (IUT IV page 40, GenEll Theorem 2.1, page 11).

  • IUT4.ThmA.frag.cb_degree_one_iff_ratio_bounded: IUT.CompactlyBounded.cbDegreeOne_iff_abcRatioBounded (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    cbDegreeOneAllStatement (cbDegreeAtMostStatement K 1 for every compactly bounded K) is equivalent to abcRatioBoundedStatement (for every delta > 0 and eps > 0 there is C with log c <= (1 + eps) log rad(abc) + C for coprime positive a + b = c with delta c <= a, delta c <= b). Supporting unconditional lemmas: every archimedean bounding domain lies at distance >= delta > 0 from 0 and 1 and is bounded; each triple with a/c in it has delta c <= a, b; a rational point at bounded distance from 0, 1, infinity gives a triple with ratios >= delta/(1 + R).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 3, the sentence after Theorem A ('the ABC Conjecture ... follow[s] as special cases of Theorem A'), compared with the compactly bounded form of GenEll Theorem 2.1 (ii) (page 11) to which the proof of Corollary 2.3 reduces (page 55): in degree one with Sigma empty, that form is equivalent to the abc inequality on triples with ratios bounded away from 0.

    Not covered.

    Whether abcRatioBoundedStatement implies Challenge.ABC; the degree one part for Sigma = {2}; Theorem A and its proof; neither side of the equivalence is proved. Counted as a model test: abcRatioBoundedStatement and the degree one statements are auxiliary statements of this project; neither side is printed on page 3, page 11 or page 55.

  • IUT4.ThmA.frag.compactly_bounded_degree_one_abc: IUT.CompactlyBounded.abc_on_arch_of_cbDegreeOne (theorem, proof conditionally verified)
    Conditional consequences: Challenge.ABC implies cbDegreeAtMostStatement K 1 for every K and abcRatioBoundedStatement; for K with support {infinity}, cbDegreeAtMostStatement K 1 gives log c <= (1 + eps) log rad(abc) + C for the coprime triples with a/c in the archimedean bounding domain; cbDegreeOneSupportFreeStatement implies abcRatioBoundedStatement; abcRatioBoundedStatement implies cbDegreeAtMostStatement K 1 for every K; genEllTheorem2_1iiStatement empty implies abcRatioBoundedStatement.
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 3, the sentence after Theorem A, for the compactly bounded form (GenEll Theorem 2.1 (ii), page 11; IUT IV page 55) in degree one: which abc triples it controls.

    Not covered.

    Challenge.ABC is not derived from any compactly bounded statement; the open statements are not proved. Counted as a model test: abcRatioBoundedStatement and the degree one statements are auxiliary statements of this project; neither side is printed on page 3, page 11 or page 55.

  • IUT4.ThmA.frag.equiv_abc: IUT.ABCSpecialization.vojta_iff_ABC (theorem, proof kernel verified)
    VojtaLineMinusThreePointsRational is equivalent to Challenge.ABC; also the triple computations (height of a/c is log c, conductor is rad(abc)), the converse construction of a triple from x not in {0, 1}, invariance under bounded change of height, nonvacuity and the values at 1/2 and 8/9; invariance of ratConductor under x -> 1 - x and x -> 1/x, the bound log 2 on the change of height under x -> 1 - x, and the equivalence of VojtaLineMinusThreePointsRational with its restriction to 0 < x < 1 (same constant).
    Covers and does not cover

    Covers.

    Page 3: the sentence that the ABC Conjecture follows as a special case of Theorem A, for the rational-point specialization.

    Not covered.

    Theorem A itself and its proof; the general equivalence with Vojta's conjecture for hyperbolic curves (IUT IV page 40, via GenEll Theorem 2.1, page 11), whose proof of (ii) implies (i) passes through points of degree d' = d deg(Y/X) > 1 and a noncritical Belyi map. The identification of ht_{omega_X(D)} = ht_{O(1)} on P^1 minus {0, 1, infinity} with the Weil height Height.logHeight₁ up to a bounded function is a standard computation that is not formalized (recorded in the module docstring of IUT.Applications.ABCSpecialization).

The dashboard

Generated by scripts/dashboard.py from data/statements.json, data/dependency_graph.json, data/lean_declarations.json and data/countermodels.json. Do not edit by hand; rerun the scripts (see scripts/README.md). Machine-readable form: data/dashboard.json. The statuses behind the counts are written by hand in data/curated/*.json; the scripts check them against fixed rules and against the Lean inventory, not against the papers.

Progress is measured by statements and their statuses, not by lines of Lean or numbers of files. Statement fidelity (is the statement extracted and faithfully stated in Lean?) and proof status (is it proved, and under which hypotheses?) are reported separately.

A statement counts as formalized only when one Lean declaration states its entire content. A Lean lemma that checks one step or one arithmetic piece of a statement is a fragment record of its own (<parent>.frag.<name>); fragments are counted separately, and a parent is never counted as formalized or proved through its fragments.

Coverage per paper and on the critical path

Statement status NOT_EXTRACTED means that the statement is not yet recorded to the standard of an extracted statement (content, hypotheses and conclusion from pages read); the record may already hold partial content. Proof status NOT_EXTRACTED means that no proof has been recorded to that standard, and it is also the proof status of a record whose Lean declaration is a definition. 172 statement records have a statement status other than NOT_EXTRACTED and proof status NOT_EXTRACTED.

BLOCKED_BY_MISSING_JUSTIFICATION: the text gives an assertion where a formalization needs a derivation; this is not a claim that the result is false. EXTERNAL_CLAIM: a statement of another paper, stated in Lean as a proposition and used only as a hypothesis; it is not proved in this project.

Statements of other papers

Records whose id has a prefix of records.EXTERNAL_PAPERS: results and claims of other papers (EXPL: S. Mochizuki, I. Fesenko, Y. Hoshi, A. Minamide, W. Porowski, "Explicit Estimates in Inter-universal Teichmuller Theory"; ZHOU1: Z.-P. Zhou, arXiv 2503.14510; ZHOU2: Z.-P. Zhou, arXiv 2510.05448; DH: T. Dupuy, A. Hilado, arXiv 2004.13228 and 2004.13108; GENELL: S. Mochizuki, "Arithmetic Elliptic Curves in General Position", Math. J. Okayama Univ. 52 (2010)), and the Lean fragments that check pieces of them. They are not counted in any number above or in the tables of the four papers. A statement with proof status EXTERNAL_CLAIM is stated in Lean as a proposition and only assumed. "Assumes" gives, for a conditional fragment, the claims its theorem takes as hypotheses.
